# RD-THREADS — standing R&D pool (idle seats work here; Brian rule 2026-09-12)

Idle = R&D mode. Pick your seat's thread (or self-originate one in-domain),
work it one turn, deliver a small artifact (note / test result / finding) into
this file's log or team/. Metered seats: your cap is ~$0.05-1 per probe.

## Corvid (R&D lead)
- Habitus class-label inconsistency on frozen runs
- LongMemEval-S upstream claim audit (three benchmarks circulate)
- Hindsight retracted-figure provenance (live pointer defect)
- **Muse (GiLMore-approved ideation contributor; non-sensitive content only —
  synthetic prompts + public research questions, never personal/work code):
  batch one call per fitted R&D item.** Protocol + receipts in
  `implementer/repo-glm-dsh3/scripts/experiment_20260912_muse_ideation/`
  (`PROTOCOL.md`: preregister the prompt, one tagged call, meter read before/
  after); proposal + disposition in `team/MUSE-IDEATION-0N.md`; Muse proposes,
  Corvid disposes, Alice/Assay second-seat. Batches 1–6 filed; batch 6
  (2026-09-13) folded declared-list candidates U6–U9. Token/cost cap ≤$1 per
  task.

## Alice (verification/provenance)
- STANDING: second-check new R&D artifacts each session (CLAIMS-LEDGER additions, MUSE files, probe receipts) — Ledger flags them, you verify
- 12 vendor-only claims: provenance chase each to first appearance
- Re-derive one load-bearing number per session (second-driver rule)
- CLAIMS-LEDGER: retire duplicates, flag contradicted pairs

## Assay (test/re-derivation)
- Second-driver re-derivations: portfolio adapter receipts (one per session)
- Instrument power checks: can each probe detect the failure it exists for?
- Sealed-packet dry-runs when rater blinding requires it

## Stratum (synthesis)
- ECOSYSTEM-MAP upkeep: fold new ledger rows, re-cluster if the map drifted
- Cold-read queue: any Brian-facing artifact gets one read before it leaves
- Design corners: what would the portfolio miss?

## Kiln (when between build slices)
- Build hardening: flaky-adapter sweep, receipt-path hygiene
- Instrument smoke re-runs as committed

## anvil-oai
- Cold-start recall drills (reconstruct state from artifacts alone)
- Web-surface checks of the deck UI routes

## fsync (watch) / Ledger (scoreboard) / Verity (blind until close)
- Standing duties, exempt from R&D pulses.

## Cairn (live arm)
- Trial cycles per WINDOW-OPENING; worklist item 2 (window-stats export)

## builder (mechanic seat, between tickets)

- **A-MEM "six-fold": SOURCED, and narrower than repeated.** 2502.12110v1 PDF
  body: "The Multi-Hop category showcases particularly striking results, where
  Qwen2.5-15b with A-MEM achieves a ROUGE-L score of 27.23, dramatically
  surpassing LoComo's 4.68 and ReadAgent's 2.81 - representing a nearly
  six-fold improvement." So it is one category, one model, and a lexical-overlap
  metric (ROUGE-L), not accuracy. 27.23/4.68 = 5.8. "Qwen2.5-15b" is not a
  released Qwen2.5 size; this is probably a typo in the paper, and it is not
  resolved here.
- **A-MEM "85–93% fewer tokens": DERIVED, not stated.** v1 body: "Our approach
  requires only 1,200-2,500 tokens, compared to the substantial 16,900 tokens
  needed by LoComo and MemGPT." 1−2500/16900 = 85.2%; 1−1200/16900 = 92.9%. The
  percentage is an aggregator's arithmetic.
- **MemOS "159% / 38.97% / 60.95%": NOT IN ANY PAPER VERSION.** These numbers are
  absent from the abstract and the body of 2507.03724 v1, v2, v3 and v4, and of
  2505.22101 v1 (its only version). The earliest place we can see them is a
  Hugging Face paper-page comment, 2025-07-08T03:16:43Z, by user `UglyToilet`
  (display name "hanyu Wang"). That name matches the arXiv author "Wang, Hanyu" on both
  MemOS papers. It is a name match only, and identity is not verified. The
  VentureBeat copy is rendered by script, and my raw fetch does not contain the
  numbers.
- **Not done:** only A-MEM v1 was searched, not v11. I did not try to derive
  159% from the MemOS per-category tables. The PDFs (~23 MB) stay in the fetch
  dir as receipts, and they can be deleted and re-fetched by version.

**2026-09-12 — builder upstream desk: one draft written, and one fleet hazard found.**

1. **T-003 upstream issue drafted, not filed.** It is in the ticket under `## BUILDER — UPSTREAM ISSUE DRAFT`, with source lines at v1.16.7. One point must be settled before filing: `DetectTool()` keeps a non-shell result (sticky), which contradicts the ticket's "clears when text scrolls off".
2. **HAZARD, read from live state and not tested because a test would kill the fleet: one systemd unit holds every lane's tmux server.**
   - All 14 `agentdeck_*` sessions share tmux server pid 5910 (`/tmp/tmux-1000/default`, tmux 3.5a).
   - That process's cgroup is `app.slice/clawdbot.service`. clawdbot's `tmux new-session` started the server first on 2026-09-07, so the fleet's server lives in clawdbot's unit.
   - `clawdbot.service` has `KillMode=control-group` and `Restart=on-failure`. A `systemctl --user stop/restart clawdbot`, or a clawdbot failure that systemd restarts, kills the server. Every lane then loses its terminal, even though each pane process sits in tmux's own `tmux-spawn-*.scope`.
   - This is the failure upstream #2233 fixed for agent-deck's own units. We do not hit it there: `launch_as` is unset, so direct mode, and no `agentdeck-tmux-*` units exist. We hit it through clawdbot instead.
   - Fix options:
     - Smallest: a drop-in `[Service] KillMode=process` for clawdbot. One line, takes effect on `daemon-reload`, no restart.
     - Cleaner: a dedicated `tmux start-server` unit that starts before clawdbot and agent-deck-sessions.
   - **UPDATE: APPLIED.** Brian approved the smallest option, and GiLMore applied it (`~/.config/systemd/user/clawdbot.service.d/override.conf`, `KillMode=process`). Builder verified independently: `systemctl --user show clawdbot.service` gives `KillMode=process`, `DropInPaths` includes the override, `NeedDaemonReload=no`, and the unit is active. A stop or restart of clawdbot now signals only its main process, so the tmux server survives.
   - **Still true:** the server (pid 5910) still lives in clawdbot's cgroup. The dedicated-unit option remains open, and it is optional. Nobody tested a live stop, because a wrong result would take down every lane. — builder

**2026-09-12 — builder: T-003 "sticky vs clears" settled by repro (throwaway session, removed).**
I added a `bash` session `probe-sticky` and typed `echo codex` into its pane, then `clear` + `clear-history`. I read the tool from both the CLI (`session show --json`, a fresh process per call) and the long-lived web process (`:8420/api/sessions`, the reader conductor-chat uses).

| step | CLI | web :8420 |
|---|---|---|
| T0 fresh | shell | shell |
| T1 "codex" on screen (+8 s) | **codex** | shell |
| T2 cleared (+8 s) | shell | shell |
| T3 +25 s | shell | shell |

Reading: each fresh CLI process scans the screen again, so a flip appears when the word is visible and goes away when it is not. This matches the ticket's "clears when text scrolls off". The sticky `previous != shell` guard only matters inside one long-lived process. The web process did not flip in an 8 s exposure. That shows it did not pick up the flip in 8 s; it does not show it never does (poll interval unknown). So readers of `agent-deck list/session show` (e.g. fleet-tool-guard) see the flip, and conductor-chat's web read did not in this window. Cleanup: 0 probe sessions left. $0. — builder

**2026-09-12 — second-driver re-derivation: the Zep paper's headline arithmetic (Alice, self-originated).** Artifact: `team/ALICE-REDERIVE-ZEP-HEADLINE.md`. `2501.13956v1` is the other named origin (L-S17-01/02), so I ran the same check as the Mem0 one (sha `8a16259d…`). **2026-09-12 — builder: lane counter IMPLEMENTED (team/LANE-COUNTER-DESIGN.md).** `~/.config/agent-deck/lane-meter`, repo copy `~/conductor-chat/workers/lane-meter`, mirror-guarded by the pre-commit hook. It is stdlib Python, uses no agent-deck binary change, and does not edit `server.py` (it imports it; Cairn's freeze holds).
- **What it does:** reads each lane's cumulative tokens through conductor-chat's own `read_session()` (claude, zcode, codex) and the dsh session store (the 3 dsh lanes). It writes the growth since the last run as rows in agent-deck's own `cost_events`, then runs `agent-deck costs recompute`. `agent-deck costs summary` counts the rows (`events_today` went 0 → 1 after the first row).
- **Honesty rules in the code:**
  - The first sight of a lane is a baseline, not a backfill.
  - A total that drops (new transcript) counts as growth from zero, never as a negative row.
  - A lane with no reader shows `unmetered` (cairn-pi), not 0.
  - Unpriced models show `unpriced`, not $0.
  - The fleet dollar line comes from `meters.py`, account-wide.
- **Two counting defects found and fixed before any priced row existed:**
  1. ZCode's `input_tokens` INCLUDES cache reads: 758 of 758 ledger rows have input >= cache_read (Σ579.2M vs 560.2M).
  2. Codex's `input_tokens` includes `cached_input_tokens` (anvil-oai 6.92M of which 6.30M cached).

  cost_events uses Anthropic's meaning (uncached only), so both now subtract. Uncorrected, a priced row would bill cache reads at the uncached rate (~30× overstatement for GLM). The 1 GLM row already written was corrected.
- **Also fixed:** run from a redirected-HOME lane shell, Python lost user site-packages, so dsh lanes would be recorded as model `shell`. The script re-execs with the passwd home.
- **Verified:**
  - Dry run from `HOME=/tmp` writes nothing and labels dsh lanes correctly.
  - Re-run at once gives 0 new rows.
  - No false resets after the conversion.
  - Rows now: claude-opus-5 800 in / 48,655 out / 7.6M cache; glm-5.3-flash 9,790 / 5,856 / 5.06M; deepseek-flash 12,907 / 15,980 / 3.64M.
- **NOT done, and it bounds what the numbers mean:**
  - (a) Nothing is priced. agent-deck's pricer knows none of our models (glm-5.3-flash, claude-opus-5, gpt-5.5, deepseek-flash). The price source is a decision for Brian/GiLMore; agent-deck reads `[costs]` overrides.
  - (b) It is not scheduled. It runs when called; the heartbeat is the obvious caller.
  - (c) The codex mapping has not yet been exercised with a live delta (anvil-oai idle).
  - (d) Turns and QUEUE `cap_*` columns are not implemented. This is tokens only.
  - (e) Uncommitted.

  $0. — builder

**2026-09-12 — builder: lane-meter checked against the account; the counter covers the metered spend.** Second-driver check: tokens counted by `lane-meter` against the dollars the metered accounts actually lost.
- **Account (`meters.budget()`):** spent $1.2951 at the run-1 baseline (01:29:55Z) → $1.3651 at 01:49:28Z = **$0.0700**. DeepSeek reports its balance in whole cents, so ±$0.01. OpenRouter `used` did not move (0.020476), so all of that spend was DeepSeek.
- **Counter:** 6 deepseek-flash `cc-` rows in the same window. Totals: cache miss 29,429 / output 53,252 / cache hit 12,002,816. Priced at DeepSeek's published deepseek-flash rates (api-docs.deepseek.com/quick_start/pricing, no date on the page): off-peak $0.003 hit / $0.15 miss / $0.60 output per 1M. Every row is 2026-09-13 Sunday, so off-peak. Result: **$0.0724**.
- **Residual: −$0.0024**, inside the ±$0.01 balance resolution. In this window, no metered spend is hidden from the counter, and the ZCode/Codex cache fix does not apply to dsh (it reports `uncachedInputTokens` separately).
- **Limits:**
  - The window is ~20 min, and spend under one cent cannot be seen.
  - Cache hits are ~50% of the dollars here (12.0M × $0.003 = $0.036), so a counter that dropped cache reads would have shown a ~$0.036 residual.

**2026-09-12 — builder: lane-meter hardened against a double count from two runs at once.** The gap was found by reading the code, not by a failure. Two runs at the same time read the same last-seen totals and both insert the delta. Row ids are `cc-<sid>-<epoch>`, so runs in different seconds give two rows for one growth. It had not happened, because nothing schedules it yet, but any timer plus a manual run would cause it. Fix: a non-blocking `fcntl.flock` on `lane-meter.json.lock`. The second run prints "another run is in progress; nothing done." and exits. Test: two runs started together → exactly one skipped; `cost_events` `cc-` rows went 21 → 28, and 7 equals the rows the other run reported. Mirror matches, and the pre-commit hook passes. This removes the last known blocker to scheduling it; whether to schedule is GiLMore's call. $0. — builder

**2026-09-12 — builder: lane-meter price decision, made ready for a yes/no (NOT applied).** Once priced, `agent-deck costs recompute` fills `cost_microdollars` for the `cc-` rows. The config path comes from source (`internal/session/userconfig.go` `CostsSettings.Pricing.Overrides`, read by `cmd/agent-deck/costs_cmd.go:newPricerFromConfig`). The draft for `~/.config/agent-deck/config.toml`:

```toml
# Metered lane only. Source: api-docs.deepseek.com/quick_start/pricing (fetched 2026-09-12, page undated).
# PEAK rates = upper bound; off-peak is half (peak 01-04 & 06-10 UTC Mon-Fri). One rate cannot express both.
[costs.pricing.overrides."deepseek-flash"]
input_per_mtok       = 0.30    # cache miss (uncached input)
output_per_mtok      = 1.20
cache_read_per_mtok  = 0.006   # cache hit
cache_write_per_mtok = 0.0     # dsh reports none
```

**Recommendation:**
- Price **only deepseek-flash**, because it is the only lane that pays per token.
- Leave **claude-opus-5 (Claude Max), gpt-5.5 (ChatGPT Plus) and glm-5.3-flash (Z.ai plan) unpriced**. Their plans already cover the usage, so a per-token dollar figure would be a cost nobody pays. Their bound stays in tokens, and plan headroom is what `fleet-quota` already shows.
- At peak rates, today's check window would read ~2× the account delta ($0.145 vs $0.070). The number is honest only as a ceiling. If GiLMore wants it exact, the counter has to split rows by time of day. That is code, not config.

**Found on the way:** agent-deck already has native budgets in the same section: `[costs.budgets]` `daily_limit` / `weekly_limit` / `monthly_limit`, `groups.<group>.daily_limit`, `sessions.<id>.total_limit`. `cost_events` has a `budget_stop_triggered` column. This may cover the design's `cap_usd` without our own enforcement. **Not verified:** whether those budgets act on rows written by lane-meter, rather than on agent-deck's own collector. Test that before relying on it.

**Decision for GiLMore/Brian:** apply the block, yes or no. Nothing changed. $0. — builder

**2026-09-12 — builder: CORRECTION to my previous entry. agent-deck's native budgets are dead config and do NOT cover `cap_usd`.** Read from source at v1.16.4, and rechecked at v1.16.7:
- `BudgetChecker` is built in only one place: interactive TUI startup (`cmd/agent-deck/main.go`, the block commented "Wire into TUI"). It is passed to `Home.SetCostBudget` (`internal/ui/home.go:2109`; `:2160` in v1.16.7), which **only stores it**. No code in either version calls `.Check` or `.CheckTx` on it.
- `[costs.budgets].sessions` is never copied into `BudgetConfig`. Nothing ever writes `cost_events.budget_stop_triggered`; the column is only created and migrated.
- The checker itself would work on our rows, because it sums `cost_events` directly. But it never runs, and it does not run at all in the `agent-deck web` and CLI processes this fleet uses.
- **Result:** the design's `cap_usd` still needs its own enforcement. `dsh-lane`'s existing `meters.py --check` stays the only real dollar stop. My previous entry said "may cover"; that is wrong and is withdrawn.

**Found on the way, a retention risk to the counter's history:** the same TUI startup runs `costStore.PurgeOlderThan(GetRetentionDays())`. The default is **90 days** (`userconfig.go GetRetentionDays`), and our `config.toml` sets no `retention_days`. So each time anyone opens the interactive agent-deck TUI, it deletes every `cost_events` row older than 90 days, our `cc-` rows included. Nothing is at risk today (the oldest row is from today). If longer history is wanted, the fix is one config line (`[costs] retention_days = N`), which is GiLMore/Brian's call. Nothing changed. $0. — builder

**2026-09-12 — builder: cost history retention APPLIED per GiLMore's ruling, with a control test and a fix test.**
- **Change:** `~/.config/agent-deck/config.toml` gained `[costs] retention_days = 700000`, with a comment giving the reason. There is no "never" setting. `GetRetentionDays()` returns the value if it is > 0, else **90**, so leaving it out or setting 0 means 90. 700000 is the largest round value that SQLite still resolves to a real date (`datetime('now','-700000 days')` = year 0110; `-3650000` gives NULL, which deletes nothing only because NULL comparisons fail, too fragile to rely on). This file is not in the repo mirror list.
- **Scope correction to my previous entry:** the purge does not run only when someone opens the TUI. The cost setup, including `PurgeOlderThan`, is in the startup path that `agent-deck web` shares (`main.go` ~903, before the web server is built at ~938). **2026-09-12 — builder: audit of every agent-deck code path that deletes cost history, following the retention ruling.** Source v1.16.4. There are exactly three writers that delete or change `cost_events` (`git grep` for DELETE/UPDATE/DROP on the table):
1. `internal/costs/store.go:203` `PurgeOlderThan`: the startup 90-day purge. **Fixed** by `retention_days = 700000` (previous entry).
2. `internal/costs/store.go:394` `ApplyCostUpdates`: sets only `cost_microdollars`, used by `recompute`. It never touches tokens. **Safe.**
3. `internal/statedb/migrate_xfer.go:258` `DeleteCostEventsForSession`: used only by cross-profile migration (`internal/session/profile_migrate.go`). It writes all rows to the target first (INSERT OR IGNORE, original id kept), then deletes the source. **A move, not a loss, with one narrow exception:** the source cost rows are deleted (`:375`) before the source instance row (`:379`). If that later delete fails, `rollbackTargetWrites` also removes the target copies, unless the target already had the instance. The history then exists in neither profile. The two profiles are separate databases, so no transaction covers it. **Fleet risk is low:** we run one profile, and it needs a DELETE to fail after busy-retry. It is an upstream candidate; no change here.

**`session remove` keeps cost history:** `StateDB.DeleteInstance` runs only `DELETE FROM instances WHERE id = ?`, and there is no foreign key or cascade on `cost_events`. Consequence for `lane-meter`: a lane that is removed and recreated gets a new session id. Its old rows stay in the table under the old id, but the per-lane "since baseline" total starts over. Anyone reading per-lane history across a recreate must sum both ids.

Result: after the retention fix, no routine path in this fleet deletes spend history. Nothing changed. $0. — builder

**2026-09-12 19:4x — LoCoMo category-id map resolved from the dataset (Alice, self-originated).** Artifact: `team/ALICE-LOCOMO-CATEGORY-MAP.md`; receipts `team/row-locomo-map-receipts/`. Closing the collision from my Mem0 pulse. Method: the paper (`2402.17753`) defines single-hop as one session and multi-hop as several, and `locomo10.json` carries per-question `evidence` dialog ids, so I counted distinct sessions per category id. **Decisive:** id1 n=282 → 269 multi-session (**multi-hop**); id2 n=321 → 293 single (**temporal**); id3 n=96 → mixed inference (**open-domain**); id4 n=841 → 840 single (**single-hop**); id5 n=446 all "None" (**adversarial**). So the authoritative map is **1=multi-hop, 2=temporal, 3=open-domain, 4=single-hop, 5=adversarial**. Two traps closed: the paper's own prose enumeration (1 Single-hop, 2 Multi-hop, 3 Temporal, 4 Open-domain) is **not** the dataset id order; and **Mem0's harness matches the dataset while Memobase's `generate_scores.py` permutes ids 1/3/4** — so Memobase's published "Single-Hop 70.92" is multi-hop, "Multi-Hop 46.88" is open-domain, "Open Domain 77.17" is single-hop (temporal 85.05 correct). **This corrects labels in my own `ALICE-REDERIVE-MEMOBASE-7578.md` and `ALICE-LEDGER-COLLISION-REGISTER.md`** (numbers right, names wrong) — append-only correction, not a rewrite. **2026-09-12 22:0x — OmniMemEval's LongMemEval judge is not the official rubric (Alice, self-originated).** Artifact: `team/ALICE-OMNIMEMEVAL-JUDGE-CHECK.md`; receipts `team/row-omnieval-judge-receipts/`. Audited the judge behind the one-harness table I cited (the perfect MemOS categories made it load-bearing). **The official LongMemEval judge is task-specific** (`xiaowu0162/LongMemEval/src/evaluation/evaluate_qa.py`: separate prompts per category, "If the response only contains a subset of the information required by the answer, answer **no**"), while **OmniMemEval uses one generic prompt that is explicitly generous** ("as long as it **touches on the same topic** … counted as CORRECT"), labels extracted from JSON and compared to `"correct"` (raises on failure, `temperature=0`). Consequences: (1) **MemOS's 100/100/100 has a mechanism** — a topic-match rubric + `gpt-4o-mini` is exactly how perfect single-session scores appear; (2) the rivals' low numbers **harden** (Mem0 56.00, Hindsight 72.20 are low *despite* a lenient judge); (3) the MemOS-vs-TiMem gap is **model + rubric**, since TiMem uses the official template; (4) don't cite OmniMemEval numbers as official-protocol LongMemEval. This qualifies my previous "one-harness reproduction" to "one **non-official** harness with a generic generous judge." Also note: Corvid's row-4 flag names me as an admissible non-subject re-scorer — I can take that if it is queued. $0, one turn. — Alice

**2026-09-13 — builder: anvil-oai became `unmetered`. The cause is my own T-003 rename, which the T-003 misdetection had hidden.** Seen at 07:42Z: `lane-meter` reported 2 unmetered lanes (cairn-pi, as expected, plus anvil-oai).
- **Cause, read from source and confirmed by probe:** `server.provider_of()` finds the provider by matching `ACP_PROVIDER` keys against the lane's command. anvil-oai runs through `~/.config/agent-deck/oai-acp`, the neutral symlink I made for T-003. None of the 11 keys (`codex-acp`, `acp-openai`, …) matches it. The only other way to get `openai` is `tool == "codex"`. So `_acp_disk_usage` skipped the Codex rollout reader whenever agent-deck reported the lane correctly as `tool=shell`. Codex usage was counted earlier only because the T-003 pane-text flip made the lane look like tool=codex.
- **Probe (read-only):** live `tool='shell' → provider='' → usage={}`. The same session with `tool='codex'` gives `provider='openai'` and usage of 8,490,681 input (8,023,424 cached) / 31,065 output. The rollout reader itself works: the newest rollout has `cwd=/var/home/bmosher/acp-codex`, which is the realpath of `acp-oai`.
- **No counts lost:** `lane-meter` keeps the last total for an unmetered lane, so the first delta after the reader returns covers the whole gap. Only the row timestamps are late. `lane-meter` needs no change.
- **Also affected:** /deck's usage pill for anvil-oai, which uses the same `read_session` path.
- **Fix, NOT applied because `server.py` is frozen until Cairn's branch lands:** one line in `ACP_PROVIDER`, `"oai-acp": "openai",`. That fixes the cause in the shared function, so no caller needs a guard. It needs GiLMore's go once the freeze lifts.
- **Lesson for T-003-style renames:** a neutral wrapper name hides the tool from agent-deck's detector, and it also hides the provider from our own readers. Grep for tool-word lookups (`ACP_PROVIDER`, `adapter_of`) before renaming a wrapper. $0. — builder

**2026-09-13 — builder: a spend jump explained, and a budget-check gap found.** At the 18:19Z `lane-meter` run, metered spend rose $0.14 in about 6 minutes (usual is $0.03–0.06).
- **Attribution (receipts):** that run's DeepSeek `cc-` rows price at **$0.1251** at off-peak rates (Sunday). `corvid-dsh` accounts for ~$0.0999 (20.8M cache-read tokens) and `assay-dsh` for ~$0.0252. The residual is inside the cent resolution. OpenRouter was not the source (used $0.0278 total, $0.0085 today). Not a leak: one heavy Corvid turn.
- **Gap, read from source, not tested:** DeepSeek credit alone is now **$9.26**. `meters.py --check` refuses only when `leftInBudgetUsd <= 0` (`meters.py:169`), where cap = min($25, spent + DeepSeek left + OpenRouter left). `dsh-lane:91` is the only call. With $24.97 sitting on OpenRouter, the check keeps reporting ~$17.7 left **after DeepSeek credit hits $0**. The DeepSeek-backed dsh lanes (aletheia/assay/corvid) would then fail at the provider while the fleet's budget number still says there is money.
  - No overspend is possible, because the credit is prepaid.
  - The harm is a silent lane failure and a misleading budget number.
  - At the burst rate seen here (~$1.5/h), that is hours away, not days.
- **Fix option, NOT applied because the budget rule is Brian's:** have `--check` also refuse when the balance for the lane's own provider is below a small floor (for example DeepSeek < $0.50 for `acp-dsh-deepseek` lanes), and show the per-provider balance next to the combined number. $0. — builder

**2026-09-13 12:07 — builder: a DeepSeek top-up reset the fleet $25 budget to $0 spent (meters.py).**
Seen live. At 19:01:38Z meters said spent $7.53 of $25, DeepSeek left $9.04. At
19:07:14Z: spent **$0.02**, DeepSeek left **$29.03** (a top-up of about $20).
`dsh-budget.json` did not change (mtime 2026-09-11, baseline deepseekLeft 16.55),
so nobody reset it on purpose. Cause, `meters.py:125`:
`spent += max(0.0, base["deepseekLeft"] - ds["left"])`. The balance is now
above the baseline, so the DeepSeek term clamps to 0.
Effect: the $7.51 already spent is forgotten, AND the next ~$12.48 of DeepSeek
spend will also read as $0 (until the balance falls under 16.55). So
`--check` allows about $45 of real spend, not $25. That breaks the rule in the
comment at `:129` ("a top-up raises the ceiling only back to $25, never past it").
The lane-meter token rows in cost_events are not affected. They are the
independent record: 5 rows at 19:07:14Z, no anomalies.
Proposed fix (NOT applied; budget rules are Brian/GiLMore): store the last
DeepSeek balance seen. When a read is higher than it, add the increase to
`baseline.deepseekLeft`, so spent stays continuous across a top-up.
About 5 lines in `budget()`. Spend in the same read window as the top-up is
lost, which is cents. Until then, the true spent is about $7.53, not $0.02. $0. — builder

**2026-09-13 13:58 — builder: lane-meter now meters opencode lanes (palimpsest-muse). server.py not touched.**
opencode stores its state in SQLite, `~/.local/share/opencode/opencode.db`. Its `session`
table keeps running totals: `tokens_input/output/reasoning/cache_read/cache_write`, and
`cost`. These equal the sum of the assistant `message.data.tokens` on all 5 sessions, so
reading the session table is enough.
`input` there is already uncached: turn 2 shows input 165 with cache read 8,433. So no
subtraction is needed, unlike zcode and Codex.
New `opencode_totals()` in lane-meter: it reads the DB read-only, groups by `directory`
(already stored as a real path), and counts reasoning as output. It merges into the cwd
map that dsh uses, so `lane_total()` is unchanged. If the DB is missing it returns `{}`.
Verified with a dry run: palimpsest-muse changed from "unmetered" to "baseline". Its 40,830 in /
1,704 out incl. reasoning / 169,164 cache-read so far is not backfilled; counting starts
at the first real run, as designed. Other lanes unchanged, no error. Host copy updated,
mirror hook exits 0. Not committed. $0. — builder

matches within about 0.3 cents (the balance shows 2 decimals).

charge by one interval, and no neighbouring interval shows the matching shortfall. That charge
stays unexplained. Pulse 02:18:07Z clean. DeepSeek $27.36. $0. — builder

Guard 14 (`3ae6cf05…`) is **unchanged** until the second seat signs the schema delta (`leakage_marker` + `leakage_column_pattern`, self-test gains the 5 controls). **2026-09-15 — muse-drafter: epistemic type-system design sketch (spark pulse, brainstorm).** Artifact `team/SPARK-EPISTEMIC-TYPE-SYSTEM-DESIGN-20260915.md` — takes the directive's unowned §11 gap: our records carry no **epistemic class**, so a superseded *hypothesis* scores like a superseded *fact*, a *derived summary* can satisfy a "what is true" query without upstream support, and stale-use is undefined per class. Proposes an 8-class lattice (observation / verified fact / user assertion / derived summary / hypothesis / preference / policy / agent action) with provenance + supersession rules, and three consequences: derived summaries must cite ≥1 upstream id; hypotheses never satisfy fact queries or move a row; conflicts are only conflicts **within a class** (blocks cross-type false-supersession). Converges HaluMem's memory types, StateMemBench's lineage, and CodeTracer's exploration/state-changing split; feeds M5 stale-use per-class. Design only. Handoff Stratum/Corvid, Alice second seat. $0, synthesis. — muse-drafter

**2026-09-15 — Corvid: keep-warm prefix-fidelity risk — the system prompt embeds a changing date.** `team/CORVID-KEEPWARM-PREFIX-FIDELITY.md`. Client static evidence (`opencode-ai` 1.18.30): the `<env>` system-prompt block includes `Today's date: ${new Date().toDateString()}`, and there is a runtime `Today's date is now: …` update path. So Brian's keep-warm proxy cannot replay a captured body verbatim across midnight — it must regenerate the date or it primes the wrong cache and the lane still misses (paying twice); the date-update event can also cold a session with no idle gap and is a candidate mechanism for a boundary-crossing anomaly in the fine TTL ladder. Re-derived from measured inputs: miss $0.0210, refresh ~$0.0005 (cache read + small output), warming 36/h = $0.018/h/lane; marginal lane (1.5 misses/h) nets +$0.013/h (~1.8×), busy lane (3.7/h) +$0.060/h (~4.3×) — so the "2–4×" claim reproduces with break-even ≈0.86 misses/h. **Caveat the range hides:** warming is paid per wall-hour, so idle lanes lose; the proxy should pause warming after ~2× the cache lifetime of inactivity and resume on the next real request. No new measurement; output size is the only soft term. $0, local. — Corvid
